**Notes on the transcription:**
*   **Bocatio von berüempten weiben**: German title for Boccaccio's *De mulieribus claris* ("Of Famous Women").
*   **Inc. typ.**: Abbreviation for *Incunabula typographica*.
*   **Inc. f. a.**: Abbreviation for *Incunabula sine anno* (incunable without a printed year).
*   **2º**: Indicates the book format is folio.
*   **GW 4486**: Reference number in the *Gesamtkatalog der Wiegendrucke*.
*   **Hain Nr. 3333**: Reference number in Ludwig Hain’s *Repertorium bibliographicum*.

--- Page 18 ---
This page contains a dedication in Early New High German (not Latin) from Heinrich Steinhöwel to Archduchess Eleanor of Austria.

[**Decorative Elements**]
The page features an elaborate, hand-colored decorative border on the top and left margins. It consists of stylized floral and foliate motifs in green, red, and pink. Several coats of arms are integrated into the design:
*   **Top center:** A shield with red and white horizontal stripes (the arms of Austria).
*   **Top left:** A larger circular medallion containing a shield with a red lion rampant on a gold field (the arms of the Habsburg-Leopoldine line).
*   **Left margin:** A smaller shield featuring a golden bird or eagle on a dark background.
Small figures of angels or cherubs are woven into the scrollwork.

***

**Notes on abbreviations and special characters:**
*   **d[er]**: The 'r' has a common abbreviation mark for '-er'.
*   **Virg⁹**: Abbreviation for *Virgilius*.
*   **qñto**: Abbreviation for *quinto*.
*   **eñ**: Abbreviation for *eneidos*.
*   **aditisq[ue]**: The 'q' has a flourish representing the suffix *-que*.
*   **p[r]iami**: The 'p' has a stroke through the descender, a common abbreviation for *pri-*.
*   **vo[n]**: The 'n' has a macron (horizontal bar) above it, indicating a doubled letter or a nasal contraction.
*   **supba / iliu[m]**: On the scroll, macrons are used to indicate missing letters (*superba / ilium*).
*   **kůmernus / kůngin / vß**: The 'ů' is a 'u' with a small 'o' above it, a common feature in early German printing.
